# Digest 20261009-060601 (2026-10-09)

**Searches run:** lung-screening, lung-nordic, lung-vaccines-cell, lung-local-therapy (new searches, on-demand backfill `--since 2026-01-01`).

**Errors:** none. All sources returned `ok`. **Truncation:** no query was flagged `truncated` (both ClinicalTrials.gov queries returned well under the 500 cap). The Helsedirektoratet guideline page could not be opened from this environment (DNS failure) when checking what changed in the May 2026 revision.

**Counts:**
- lung-screening: PubMed, window 2026/01/01 to 2026/10/09: 1457 found, 885 new
- lung-local-therapy: PubMed, window 2026/01/01 to 2026/10/09: 1165 found, 620 new
- lung-local-therapy: ClinicalTrials.gov registrations/updates (cond "lung cancer", stereotactic/oligometastatic/consolidative RT/segmentectomy), window 2026-01-01 to 2026-10-09: 62 found, 16 new
- lung-nordic: PubMed, window 2026/01/01 to 2026/10/09: 165 found, 99 new
- lung-nordic: web `lungekreft site:helsedirektoratet.no`: 2 kept, 2 new
- lung-nordic: web `lungekreft site:kreftregisteret.no`: 1 kept (no kreftregisteret.no pages returned; kept the FHI-hosted registry quality report), 1 new
- lung-vaccines-cell: PubMed, window 2026/01/01 to 2026/10/09: 68 found, 29 new
- lung-vaccines-cell: ClinicalTrials.gov registrations/updates (cond "lung cancer", vaccine/oncolytic/TIL), window 2026-01-01 to 2026-10-09: 25 found, 3 new

Total new: 1607 (0 practice-changing, 22 high, 312 medium, 1273 low). 48 items match more than one search; they are listed once, under the first search.

Backfill run: medium reasons are one line, and low items have no reason. Much of the screening search is AI-imaging, radon/air-pollution measurement and biomarker-discovery papers, rated low or medium.

## Practice-changing

None this run. The closest candidates fall short: Israel's decision to fund LDCT screening (from September 2025) is reported second-hand in a pilot evaluation, not by the deciding body, and is outside Norway. The European Code Against Cancer 5th edition now recommends organised lung screening, but it is a public-facing code rather than a national programme or clinical guideline decision. The May 2026 revision of the Norwegian handlingsprogram may contain changed recommendations, but the item does not say which, and the page could not be checked; it is rated high and should be re-rated if a change is confirmed. The CALGB 140503 and JCOG1211 items are secondary analyses of trials that already changed practice.

## High

### lung-screening: publications
- **Norwegian lung cancer screening pilot (TIDL): baseline results.** All 125,095 residents aged 60–79 in Akershus were invited. Ever-smokers were selected with a PLCOm2012NoRace questionnaire (≥35 pack-years or ≥2.6% 6-year risk). Of 2,499 eligible, 1,006 had baseline LDCT (Aug 2022–May 2023) in a radiology-led model with Lung-RADS v2022. 23 cancers were found (2.3%), 83% stage I and 9% stage II, mostly treated with robotic surgery. Only 2.9% were referred onwards; false-positive rate was 0.6% after pulmonologist referral and 1.7% after radiological staging; 13.8% needed 3- or 6-month follow-up CT. This is the first Norwegian screening data and supports a national programme, but it covers one county, one round and a small, self-selected group (40% of eligibles attended), with no mortality data. Mahovkic A, Neumann K, Strand TE et al. *Acta Oncol* 2026. [PMID 41631590](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41631590/)
- **Stockholm risk-based screening pilot: socioeconomic disparities in participation.** Registry-linked cohort of 34,592 women aged 55–74 invited in Region Stockholm (2022–2024). 33% completed the risk questionnaire, 9.6% of those were eligible, and 89% of eligibles attended LDCT. Questionnaire response was higher with higher education (RR 1.71), income (RR 1.72) and better area conditions (RR 1.59), while the disadvantaged were more often eligible; attendance among eligibles was largely equitable. The lesson for a Norwegian programme is that the invitation/risk-assessment step, not the CT, is where inequity arises. Women only, one region, early pilot. Delilovic S, Yacaman Mendez D, Markholm Nordgren N et al. *JAMA Netw Open* 2026. [PMID 42461629](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42461629/)
- **Israel national TIGAR screening pilot (2021–2024) and funding decision.** 5,395 heavy current/former smokers were screened through all four HMOs; participation 50.5% among eligibles, 63 cancers (1.2%), 58% stage I–II. PPV was 11.6% for Lung-RADS ≥3 and 54% for 4B/4X; false-positive rate 8.4% (Lung-RADS ≥3); median 6.4 weeks to diagnostic procedure. The authors report that LDCT screening for ages 65–74 entered the national health basket in September 2025, with expansion to 60–74 in 2026. Limits: early-stage share is modest, smoking documentation incomplete, no mortality data. Maya R, Liora V, Dorith S et al. *Lung Cancer* 2026. [PMID 42456639](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42456639/)
- **European Code Against Cancer, 5th edition: organised screening programmes.** The ECAC5 (IARC/EU) adds lung cancer to its screening recommendations: annual LDCT for people at increased risk by age, smoking history or validated risk models, with biennial screening as an alternative, always combined with smoking cessation. It strengthens the European case for programmes like the Norwegian pilot, but it is a recommendation to the public and policymakers, not a national decision. Toes-Zoutendijk E, Arbyn M, Auvinen A et al. *Mol Oncol* 2026. [PMID 41542798](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41542798/)
- **ITALUNG + LUSI pooled, with NLST: screening effect by sex and histology.** Extended pooled analysis of two European RCTs (7,258 participants) compared with and combined with NLST (53,452). Combined lung-cancer mortality HR was 0.74 (95% CI 0.61–0.90) in women vs 0.93 (0.80–1.06) in men at 6 years (p for difference 0.06). Mortality fell for non-lepidic adenocarcinoma (HR 0.80) and other NSCLC (0.66) in both sexes, not for squamous cell carcinoma; SCLC mortality fell in women only (HR 0.63, 0.43–0.94). Post-hoc, letter format, few events per subtype; the SCLC finding is hypothesis-generating. Kaaks R, Cortés-Ibáñez FO, Delorme S et al. *Cancer Commun (Lond)* 2026. [PMID 41743631](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41743631/)
- **BioMILD trial: ~10-year outcomes of risk-adapted LDCT intervals.** Prospective trial of 4,119 heavy smokers; those with negative baseline LDCT were rescreened every 3 years. Over median 9.4 years, 248 cancers were diagnosed: 21.8% in baseline LDCT-positive vs 3.0% in LDCT-negative participants; 10-year lung-cancer mortality 5% vs 1%. About half of cancers were stage I in every interval, and adherence was 92% at 3–5 years and 58% beyond 9 years. Supports extended intervals after a negative baseline scan, but cancers keep appearing in the low-risk group and there is no randomised comparison with annual screening. Ledda RE, Sabia F, Beck KS et al. *Lung Cancer* 2026. [PMID 42777510](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42777510/)
- **Risk models across racial and ethnic groups: Lung Cancer Cohort Consortium.** 641,830 US ever-smokers from 12 cohorts; 16 risk models evaluated. Most models underestimated risk in non-Hispanic Black participants and discriminated worse in Asian and Black participants. At the same eligible fraction as USPSTF-2021 (38%), all risk-model strategies were more efficient; PLCOm2012 (mean NNS 36.5) and LYFS-CT (40.1) performed best. Supports model-based eligibility, as used in the Norwegian pilot; limited by small Asian and Hispanic samples and US-only data. Feng X, Guida F, Guenoun A et al. *Ann Intern Med* 2026. [PMID 42372272](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42372272/)
- **Breathomics triage tool, prospective multicentre validation (n=5,214).** Symptomatic patients with radiological lung abnormalities had exhaled VOCs analysed by PTR-TOF-MS; a model combining VOCs and clinical factors was locked and blind-validated. External AUC 0.85 (95% CI 0.81–0.89); at the rule-out threshold sensitivity 93.1%, specificity 55.9%, NPV 71% (89% in the pulmonary-clinic subgroup). The largest breath-test validation so far, but in a Chinese symptomatic population, not a screening cohort, and specificity is modest. Qin J, Sun M, Li X et al. *Chest* 2026. [PMID 42521150](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42521150/)
- **L5MetP: five-metabolite blood signature for earlier detection (PLCO).** Prediagnostic sera from 1,259 cases (within 5 years of diagnosis) and 8,470 ever-smoker controls. The panel (diacetylspermine, creatine riboside, arginine, hypoxanthine, acylcarnitine C18:1) gave AUC 0.79 (0.76–0.82) for 1-year risk and added to existing clinical risk models. A candidate pre-screening tool; it needs prospective validation and a demonstrated impact on screening selection. Irajizad E, Rudsari HK, Gendarme S et al. *Cell Rep Med* 2026. [PMID 42777710](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42777710/)
- **Taiwan: national stage shift and self-initiated LDCT in a never-smoker-dominated population.** Taiwan Cancer Registry 2011–2023 (126,753 adenocarcinomas; 69% never-smokers) linked to 70,803 opportunistic LDCT screenees (84% never-smokers). Stage 0 (AAPC +49.6%) and stage I (+13.0%) incidence rose; late-stage incidence fell only in some groups, such as never-smoking women <55; incidence-based mortality declined. The most direct population data on screening never-smokers, but ecological: overdiagnosis and benefit cannot be separated. Wu JJ, Chiang CJ, Chen CH et al. *Lancet Reg Health West Pac* 2026. [PMID 42239973](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42239973/)
- **PM2.5 constituents and lung cancer in Chinese never-smokers (China Kadoorie Biobank).** 262,785 never-smokers, 2,646 lung cancers. Per IQR, nitrate (HR 1.35, 1.23–1.48), ammonium (1.22) and chloride (1.20) were associated with higher risk; the overall mixture HR was 1.13; nitrate interacted with a polygenic risk score. Large prospective aetiology study; exposure is modelled from residence and residual confounding (e.g. cooking fumes) is possible. Xia Z, Han Y, Mou Y et al. *J Hazard Mater* 2026. [PMID 42828982](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42828982/)

### lung-nordic: publications
- **CONOR: time from smoking debut to lung cancer in Norway.** Linked data from 11 Norwegian population cohorts (80,761 ever-smokers, 1,336 lung cancers). Median time from smoking debut to diagnosis was 53.3 years and median age at diagnosis 72.5; lag was shorter in women (48.6 vs 55.5 years) and current smokers (50.7 vs 54.2). The authors argue this bears on age limits for Norwegian screening and on planning. Smoking history is self-reported and follow-up from cohort entry is only ~12 years. Nguyen OTD, Fotopoulos I, Tsamardinos I et al. *ERJ Open Res* 2026. [PMID 42835565](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42835565/)
- **Palliative ICI by ECOG performance status: Swedish registry.** 1,956 patients given palliative ICI in Southeast Sweden (2010–2025), 854 with lung cancer; 29% had PS ≥2. Median OS was 23.4 months at PS 0–1, 9.6 at PS 2 and 3.8 at PS 3, and 46% of PS 3 patients died within 90 days. The authors suggest refraining from ICI at PS 3. Observational and confounded by indication; results are not split by tumour type for PS. Nodbrant P, Ulff E, Lagerlund M et al. *Acta Oncol* 2026. [PMID 42397290](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42397290/)
- **Real-world ICI use and survival in Sweden 2015–2023.** Population-based study from 10 regions (4.3 million people) using a new drug-record source linked to registers. Median OS after palliative ICI was 2.2 years for lung cancer (n=1,941 palliative across diagnoses); ICI use reached 6.7% of all systemic cancer treatment in 2023, with regional differences in adoption. A useful Nordic benchmark for Norwegian outcomes; limited clinical detail (stage, PD-L1, line). Illergard K, Soto P, Arpegård J et al. *Ups J Med Sci* 2026. [PMID 42729917](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42729917/)

### lung-nordic: web
- **Lungekreft, mesoteliom og thymom – handlingsprogram (Helsedirektoratet).** The national guideline for lung cancer, mesothelioma and thymoma. The search snippet states it was last revised in May 2026, with updates on targeted and immunotherapy drugs. Which recommendations changed could not be verified (the page was unreachable from this environment), so it is rated high, not practice-changing; worth checking the revision notes by hand. [helsedirektoratet.no](https://www.helsedirektoratet.no/retningslinjer/lungekreft-mesoteliom-og-thymom-handlingsprogram/innledning)

### lung-local-therapy: publications
- **PACIFIC-R: 5-year real-world outcomes with durvalumab after chemoradiotherapy.** International observational cohort (n=1,153, early-access programme 2017–2018, including Nordic sites). Median rwPFS 24.3 months (5-year 35.2%) and median OS 59.0 months (5-year 49.2%, 95% CI 46.2–52.2), comparable to PACIFIC. Outcomes held after sequential CRT (median OS 47.1 months) and with PD-L1 <1% (43.3 months). Confirms the standard in routine care; retrospective chart extraction, and patients who progressed before starting durvalumab are not included. Girard N, Bar J, Baas P et al. *ESMO Open* 2026. [PMID 41643268](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41643268/)
- **CALGB 140503: outcomes by age, lobar vs sublobar resection.** Secondary analysis of the randomised trial (n=697; 129 aged >75). Grade ≥3 adverse events and 90-day mortality were similar across age groups with both operations; older patients had lower DFS and OS mainly from competing risks (e.g. >75 vs <65 after lobectomy, OS HR 2.73). Supports offering surgery, including sublobar resection, to fit older patients rather than defaulting to SBRT. Post-hoc and underpowered within age-by-procedure subgroups. Ganti AK, Damman B, Wang X et al. *Clin Lung Cancer* 2026. [PMID 42501610](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42501610/)
- **JCOG1211: omitting mediastinal node dissection in segmentectomy for GGO-dominant tumours.** Ad hoc PSM analysis (127 pairs) within the single-arm phase 3 JCOG1211. Lobe-specific vs no mediastinal dissection: 5-year OS and RFS 98.4% vs 99.2%; one pN1 upstaging and no pN2; complications 47.2% vs 29.9% (p=0.005), atrial fibrillation 3.1% vs 0%. Supports de-escalating nodal dissection for GGO-dominant cT1N0 disease; non-randomised, surgeon's choice, very few events. Onodera K, Aokage K, Saji H et al. *Eur J Cardiothorac Surg* 2026. [PMID 42341065](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42341065/)

### lung-local-therapy: new trial registrations
- **Thoracic radiotherapy to the primary in non-oligometastatic stage IV NSCLC.** Randomised phase 3 (registered as "NA"), radical 3D RT to the primary plus systemic therapy vs systemic therapy alone in patients with >5 metastases; endpoints PFS and local control. Not yet recruiting, n=200, Guizhou Medical University. [NCT07462728](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07462728)
- **FSRT + bevacizumab vs hippocampal-avoidant WBRT-SIB for extensive brain metastases from lung adenocarcinoma.** Phase 3, 1:1; FSRT 30 Gy/5 plus bevacizumab ×4 vs HA-WBRT 25 Gy with SIB 40 Gy/10; endpoints intracranial control and neurocognition. Recruiting, n=220, Sun Yat-sen University. [NCT07481786](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07481786)

### lung-vaccines-cell: publications
- **OVM-200 survivin peptide vaccine: first-in-human phase 1a.** 12 patients with NSCLC, ovarian or prostate cancer received 3 doses (250–2,000 µg, 3+3 design). No dose-limiting toxicities; all AEs were grade 1 injection-site reactions. At 2,000 µg, median anti-survivin IgG titre was 1:327,680 and ELISpot 1,282 SFU/10⁶ cells; stable disease in 6/12 (3/3 at the top dose), some short-lived. Phase 1b is under way at 2,000 µg. Tiny, mixed-tumour, company-funded; no efficacy conclusions possible, and the number of lung cancer patients is not stated in the abstract. Wijaya W, Morris T, Forster MD et al. *EClinicalMedicine* 2026. [PMID 41536940](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41536940/)

### lung-vaccines-cell: new trial registrations
- **LabVax 3(22)-23 (labyrinthin peptide vaccine) + GM-CSF, then with pembrolizumab, in advanced lung adenocarcinoma.** Phase 2, not yet recruiting, n=30, VA Office of Research and Development. [NCT07859592](https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07859592)
